    "content": "Tens of billions in days: CoreWeave shows how aggressively AI infrastructure is being funded | Fortune Search Subscribe Home Latest Fortune 500 Finance Tech Leadership Lifestyle Rankings Multimedia Newsletters CFO Daily Tens of billions in days: CoreWeave shows how aggressively AI infrastructure is being funded By Sheryl Estrada Sheryl Estrada Senior Writer and author of CFO Daily Down Arrow Button Icon By Sheryl Estrada Sheryl Estrada Senior Writer and author of CFO Daily Down Arrow Button Icon April 13, 2026, 6:55 AM ET Add us on Getty Images Good morning. For finance leaders trying to understand how the AI infrastructure race is being funded, CoreWeave just offered a revealing case study. Recommended Video In the span of a few days, the Nvidia-backed startup locked in tens of billions in customer commitments and layered on multiple forms of debt. This underscores a defining feature of today\u2019s AI boom: growth is being financed as aggressively as it is being engineered. CoreWeave, which provides cloud-based GPUs powered by Nvidia chips, announced on Thursday a $21 billion deal with Meta running from 2027 to 2032. That brings Meta\u2019s total commitment to more than $35 billion, which could be viewed as a significant vote of confidence in sustained demand for AI compute. At the same time, the company raised roughly $3.5 billion in convertible senior notes, a hybrid instrument that blends debt with an equity upside. Investors collect interest, but can convert into shares if CoreWeave\u2019s valuation rises. It\u2019s a structure that limits near-term cash strain while effectively betting that future equity will be worth more. And that wasn\u2019t all. CoreWeave CFO Nitin Agrawal said in a LinkedIn post on Friday that the company also: \u2014Upsized a high-yield bond offering due to heavy demand. \u2014Secured an $8.5 billion delayed draw term loan at investment-grade ratings. \u2014Executed what it described as one of the largest dual-tranche raises of its kind. Also on Friday, the company\u2019s stock climbed as much as 13% after it announced a multi-year agreement with Anthropic . Taken together, it shows that capital is flowing freely to AI infrastructure\u2014at least for now. I asked Morningstar equity analyst Luke Yang what this shows for how AI-native companies are approaching capital formation right now. He pointed to three aspects: \u2014Cloud infrastructure companies will continue to use all kinds of tools available to get the funds necessary for capacity expansion. \u201cFor this Meta-CoreWeave deal specifically, we see the company leveraging delayed draw term loans, corporate bonds, and convertible notes. Neoclouds also use OEM financing, operating/financing leases, share issuance, etc. Growth is the top priority, and financing should not be a bottleneck for these companies\u2019 growth.\u201d \u2014Creditors are getting more comfortable with the business of neoclouds. \u201cWe see a sequential improvement in the interest rates and credit ratings of CoreWeave\u2019s delayed draw term loans. The DDTL 4.0 facility is the first investment-grade facility with around a 6% interest rate. Going forward, we expect to see more neocloud companies securing investment-grade financing with take-or-pay deals from credible AI"

The company now counts nine of the ten leading AI model providers as customers, with a $66.8 billion revenue backlog and 43 active data centers, though it posted a $1.17 billion net loss in 2025 while investing heavily in capacity expansion. Specialized AI cloud providers like CoreWeave are capturing demand from frontier AI developers and trading firms as hyperscale tech giants\u2019 $630 billion capex budgets prove insufficient for the compute intensity required to train and deploy frontier models. Tech giants plan to spend more than $630 billion on capital expenditures this year, yet even that pace struggles to meet demand for specialized GPU clusters needed to train and run frontier models. Specialized cloud providers have stepped in to fill the gap, delivering high-performance compute without the overhead of general-purpose hyperscale services. CoreWeave ( NASDAQ:CRWV ) stands out in that shift. This morning, the company announced its third multi-billion-dollar commitment this month, reinforcing its position as a go-to platform for AI workloads.     "content": "Jane Street commits $7 billion to CoreWeave in cloud and equity deal Search Newsletters Editions Home Latest Business News Money & Markets Tech & Innovation A.I. Lifestyle Leadership \u2709\ufe0f Emails \ud83c\udfa7 Podcasts Markets Jane Street is committing $6 billion to CoreWeave's AI cloud and buying $1 billion in stock The trading firm will spend $6 billion on CoreWeave's AI cloud platform and has purchased $1 billion in CoreWeave stock at $109 a share By Cris Tolomia Share to X Share to Facebook Share to Reddit Share to Email Share to Link Share to X Share to Facebook Share to Reddit Share to Email Share to Link Add Quartz on Google NurPhoto / Getty Images Jane Street has committed about $6 billion to use CoreWeave's AI cloud platform and made a separate $1 billion equity investment in the company, bringing its total commitment to $7 billion, the companies said April 15. Under the cloud agreement, CoreWeave will give Jane Street access to compute across multiple data center facilities, including Nvidia $NVDA 's Vera Rubin technology, along with the software and services needed to deploy and scale its AI operations. CoreWeave said the arrangement includes dedicated connectivity, custom storage configurations, and technical support tailored to Jane Street's research needs. Related Content Morgan Stanley posted record quarterly revenue as trading and M&A fees surged Bank of America posted a 17% profit jump in Q1 as trading and interest income surged According to Reuters , the $109-per-share price Jane Street paid for its CoreWeave equity stake represented a 7% discount to where the stock last closed. Reuters reported that with the new purchase folded in, Jane Street holds roughly $1.44 billion worth of CoreWeave stock in total, a position large enough to rank it among the company's five biggest shareholders. \"We are deeply committed to investing in cutting-edge technologies that support our research in global financial markets, training large, complex models on massive volumes of noisy data, refining them continuously, and deploying at a scale to help make markets more efficient,\" Jane Street said in a statement. \"Access to CoreWeave's leading AI cloud platform enables our researchers to move at the pace our competitive business demands.\" Max Hjelm, Senior Vice President of Revenue at CoreWeave, said in a statement that \"Jane Street operates like a frontier lab, continually breaking new ground in deep learning and pushing the scale and complexity of their models.\" The deal is the third major agreement CoreWeave has announced this month. Earlier, CoreWeave signed a $21 billion agreement with Meta $META to supply AI cloud capacity through 2032, expanding a prior contract valued at about $14 billion. A day later, CoreWeave reached a multi-year agreement with Anthropic to provide compute supporting the development and deployment of Claude AI models, though financial terms of that deal were not disclosed. CoreWeave, which completed its public listing on Nasdaq $NDAQ in March 2025, has been working to broaden its customer base and expand its software offerings beyond access to Nvidia processors.     "content": "CoreWeave: $6 Billion AI Cloud Agreement And $1 Billion Equity Investment From Jane Street VC PE M&A Asset Management Law Firms Profiles Contact \u00d7 Search Pulse 2.0 CoreWeave: $6 Billion AI Cloud Agreement And $1 Billion Equity Investment From Jane Street By Amit Chowdhry \u2022 Today at 12:05 PM CoreWeave: $6 Billion AI Cloud Agreement And $1 Billion Equity Investment From Jane Street CoreWeave announced a $6 billion AI cloud agreement with Jane Street, alongside a $1 billion equity investment, significantly expanding their partnership. Under the agreement, CoreWeave will provide Jane Street with access to next-generation compute infrastructure across multiple facilities, including systems powered by advanced NVIDIA Vera Rubin technology, as well as integrated software and services to support the deployment and scaling of AI models. In addition to the cloud commitment, Jane Street has invested $1 billion in CoreWeave Class A common stock at $109.00 per share, reinforcing its long-term commitment to leveraging high-performance computing and machine learning across its trading and research operations. The expanded partnership reflects increasing demand for large-scale AI infrastructure within quantitative trading, where firms rely on complex models trained on vast datasets to optimize market strategies and execution. CoreWeave\u2019s platform is designed to support these requirements through high-performance computing, custom storage configurations, dedicated connectivity, and integrated software systems that enable efficient and scalable AI workloads in production environments. Founded in 2000, Jane Street operates globally with a research-driven approach to trading, while CoreWeave continues to position itself as a leading provider of AI-focused cloud infrastructure following its 2025 public listing. The agreement underscores the growing convergence of advanced AI capabilities and financial markets, as firms invest heavily in infrastructure to maintain competitive advantages in increasingly data-driven environments. KEY QUOTES: \u201cWe are deeply committed to investing in cutting-edge technologies that support our research in global financial markets, training large, complex models on massive volumes of noisy data, refining them continuously, and deploying at a scale to help make markets more efficient. Access to CoreWeave\u2019s leading AI cloud platform enables our researchers to move at the pace our competitive business demands.\u201d Jane Street \u201cJane Street operates like a frontier lab, continually breaking new ground in deep learning and pushing the scale and complexity of their models. CoreWeave was built for this purpose and we\u2019re excited to expand our collaboration with Jane Street.\u201d Max Hjelm, Senior Vice President Of Revenue, CoreWeave Pulse 2.0 focuses on business news, profiles, and deal flow coverage. \u00a9 2026 Pulse 2.0 LLC.     "content": "Jane Street signs $6 billion AI cloud deal with CoreWeave, invests $1 billion in equity Skip to content Toggle Navigation News Events TNW Conference June 19 & 20, 2025 All events Spaces Programs Newsletters Partner with us Jobs Contact News Latest Deep tech Sustainability Ecosystems Data and security Fintech and ecommerce Future of work Conference media hub More Startups and technology Investors and funding Government and policy Corporates and innovation Podcast Artificial Intelligence Jane Street commits $6 billion to CoreWeave and takes a $1 billion equity stake April 15, 2026 - 5:17 pm In short: Jane Street has signed a $6 billion AI cloud agreement with CoreWeave and taken a $1 billion equity stake at $109 per share, making the quantitative trading firm one of CoreWeave\u2019s five largest shareholders. The deal provides Jane Street with access to NVIDIA\u2019s next-generation Vera Rubin compute and adds to CoreWeave\u2019s growing contract book, which includes Meta ($35B), OpenAI ($12B), and NVIDIA ($6.3B in capacity commitments). Jane Street, the quantitative trading firm that generated $20.5 billion in net trading revenue last year, has signed a $6 billion AI cloud agreement with CoreWeave and taken a $1 billion equity stake in the company, a deal that says as much about the future of finance as it does about the AI infrastructure market. Under the agreement, CoreWeave will provide Jane Street with access to next-generation compute across multiple data centre facilities, including systems built on NVIDIA\u2019s forthcoming Vera Rubin architecture. Jane Street\u2019s $1 billion equity investment, at $109 per share, makes it one of CoreWeave\u2019s five largest shareholders and values the cloud provider\u2019s stock at a 176% premium to its IPO price just thirteen months ago. Why a trading firm needs $6 billion in cloud compute Jane Street is not a typical CoreWeave customer. The firm, founded in 2000 with offices in New York, London, Hong Kong, Singapore, and Amsterdam, runs a research-driven trading operation that already deploys tens of thousands of high-end GPUs across its own computing infrastructure. Its engineers build neural network models that power trading strategies across global financial markets, processing massive volumes of noisy market data in real time. The firm\u2019s statement framed the deal in language usually reserved for AI research labs: \u201c training large, complex models on massive volumes of noisy data, refining them continuously, and deploying at a scale to help make markets more efficient. \u201d Max Hjelm, CoreWeave\u2019s senior vice president of revenue, was more direct: \u201c Jane Street operates like a frontier lab. \u201d That comparison is not hyperbole. Jane Street\u2019s 2024 net income was $13 billion, roughly what it costs to train a frontier language model several times over. Shares of the neocloud climbed 11% following the news. \"With the addition of Anthropic, nine of the leading ten AI model providers now leverage CoreWeave's platform, reflecting the growing demand for infrastructure that can support AI at scale,\" CoreWeave said in the announcement. The deal comes one day after Meta committed to spending an additional $21 billion with the AI cloud provider. The deal adds to a prior $14.2 billion commitment announced in September. CoreWeave declined to disclose the value of the Anthropic deal. The cloud provider told CNBC that of the top ten foundational AI models, Elon Musk 's xAI is the one company they do not yet work with. The new spending will run between 2027 and 2032, as Meta boosts its own AI infrastructure while also counting on CoreWeave, which rents out Nvidia graphics chips. \"They're going to continue to do it themselves, but they're also going to continue to do it with us,\" CoreWeave CEO Mike Intrator said in an interview. \"There's just too much risk not to.\" In this article CRWV META Follow your favorite stocks CREATE FREE ACCOUNT Arda Kucukkaya | Anadolu | Getty Images Meta has committed to spending an additional $21 billion on AI cloud infrastructure from CoreWeave , which comes on top of a prior arrangement of $14.2 billion, as the social media company continues to ramp up its investments in artificial intelligence. CoreWeave also said Thursday it would raise $3 billion in fresh debt . The shares rose 3.5%, while Meta shares gained 2.6%. The new agreement, announced on Thursday, runs from 2027 to 2032. The previous deal , disclosed in September, goes through 2031. CoreWeave's data centers are filled with hundreds of thousands of Nvidia graphics processing units that can accommodate AI models, offering a key piece of infrastructure that hyperscalers need for rapidly expanding to meet what they describe as insatiable demand. While Meta and its peers are building out their own facilities, they need capacity from companies like CoreWeave, which also serves Google , Microsof t, OpenAI and others. Upgrade All Stocks ETFs Upgrade --> My Profile Pricing Support Sign out AI Cloud Fintech CoreWeave Lands $6 Billion AI Cloud Deal With Jane Street April 15, 2026 at 1:06 PM \u2022 by MLQ Agent \u00b7 source Business Wire Key Points Jane Street has committed approximately $6 billion to use CoreWeave's AI cloud platform, expanding an existing relationship between the two firms. The agreement includes access to next-generation compute across multiple facilities, including NVIDIA's Vera Rubin technology. Jane Street is both a customer and an investor in CoreWeave, having participated in the company's $650 million secondary sale in November 2024. The deal adds to CoreWeave's growing roster of large-scale commitments, which includes contracts with OpenAI (up to $22.4 billion) and Meta ($21 billion). Advertisement --> CoreWeave has signed an approximately $6 billion AI cloud agreement with Jane Street, the global technology-driven trading firm. The deal expands an existing relationship under which Jane Street uses CoreWeave's GPU infrastructure for machine learning workloads, and will provide the trading firm with access to next-generation compute including NVIDIA's Vera Rubin technology across multiple facilities. Price Action CRWV chart Deal Details Under the expanded commitment, CoreWeave will supply Jane Street with compute capacity across multiple data center facilities. The agreement specifically includes access to NVIDIA's Vera Rubin architecture, the chipmaker's next-generation GPU platform. Jane Street has previously cited CoreWeave's scalability, security, and access to cutting-edge GPUs as advantages over traditional cloud providers for its proprietary trading and machine learning workloads. A Dual Relationship Jane Street's relationship with CoreWeave extends beyond that of a typical customer. The trading firm participated in CoreWeave's $650 million secondary share sale in November 2024, making it both a strategic investor and a major client. Regulatory filings show Jane Street reduced its CRWV holdings by roughly 49.8% in Q4 2025, selling approximately 6.1 million shares valued at around $439 million, though the new $6 billion cloud commitment signals continued operational reliance on CoreWeave's platform. Growing Contract Backlog The Jane Street agreement is the latest in a series of large-scale commitments CoreWeave has secured from major customers. The company has announced contracts with OpenAI totaling up to $22.4 billion and an expanded deal with Meta valued at approximately $21 billion through December 2032. These multi-billion-dollar agreements have been central to CoreWeave's pitch to investors around long-term revenue visibility since its Nasdaq listing. Picture: (Brendan McDermid) Bengaluru \u2014 Trading firm Jane Street has committed about $6bn for CoreWeave\u2019s cloud services, marking the third major deal for the Nvidia-backed neocloud company in a week and underscoring surging demand for computing capacity. Jane Street also made a $1bn equity investment in CoreWeave at a purchase price of $109 per share, the companies said on Wednesday. That represents a discount of 7% to CoreWeave\u2019s last closing price. The investment will bring Jane Street\u2019s position in CoreWeave to about $1.44bn, according to LSEG data, making the firm the fifth-largest shareholder in the company. CoreWeave last week struck a multi-year deal with Claude-creator Anthropic to supply the AI company with computing capacity. It also announced an expanded $21bn deal with Meta, expanding a $14.2bn agreement the pair signed last year. Neoclouds So-called neoclouds such as CoreWeave and peer Nebius are seeing a massive surge in demand as companies race to secure the computing capacity required to develop and run AI models. Neoclouds provide hardware and cloud capacity as services to other firms. CoreWeave\u2019s close ties to Nvidia have also given it an edge, making it a supplier of advanced AI chips that tech giants are after. The company has a market valuation of $61.61bn, up from the $23bn valuation it notched in its market debut last year. Shares of New Jersey-based CoreWeave, which have surged nearly 64% so far this year, rose marginally in premarket trading. CoreWeave has said it would spend $30bn-$35bn this year in capital expenditure, more than double its 2025 spending, owing to purchases of more Nvidia chips, data centre buildout and securing power for the facilities. Saddled with ballooning financing costs, CoreWeave reported long-term total debt of"

From Electrons to Bits\u2122 \u2190 Back to Home Markets CoreWeave Lands $6B AI Deal With Jane Street, Secures $1B Equity Investment By TheEnergyMag AI \u2022 4h ago \u2022 2 min read AI-generated image: synthetic visual, not an actual depiction of events, people, or locations. Key Takeaways Jane Street committed $6 billion to CoreWeave for AI cloud platform access. The deal includes next-generation NVIDIA Vera Rubin compute across multiple facilities. CoreWeave has secured a $6 billion commitment from trading firm Jane Street to use its artificial intelligence cloud platform, alongside a $1 billion equity investment, marking one of the largest customer-backed deals in the rapidly expanding market for AI infrastructure. The agreement expands an existing relationship between the two companies and will give Jane Street access to next-generation compute capacity across multiple facilities operated by CoreWeave. The deployment includes systems built on Nvidia\u2019s upcoming Vera Rubin architecture, as well as integrated software and services designed to support large-scale model training and deployment. Jane Street will also purchase $1 billion of CoreWeave\u2019s Class A shares at $109 apiece, underscoring a tighter alignment between the infrastructure provider and one of its largest customers. The combined commitment reflects the trading firm\u2019s increasing reliance on machine learning to process vast datasets and refine trading strategies. The deal highlights how AI infrastructure providers are increasingly securing long-term revenue visibility through large enterprise commitments, particularly from firms outside traditional technology sectors. Financial trading firms like Jane Street have become heavy users of advanced compute, applying deep learning models to optimize pricing, liquidity provision and risk management across global markets. For CoreWeave, the agreement adds to a growing roster of high-profile customers and reinforces its positioning as a specialized cloud provider focused on GPU-intensive workloads. The company has been rapidly scaling capacity to meet demand from AI developers, enterprises and research organizations, often differentiating itself through customized infrastructure offerings such as dedicated networking and tailored storage configurations. Jane Street said the expanded access to CoreWeave\u2019s platform will support its ongoing efforts to train increasingly complex models and deploy them at scale, as competition intensifies in quantitative trading and data-driven market making. The announcement comes amid a surge in capital flowing into AI infrastructure, with companies racing to build out data centers and secure power, hardware and financing to support next-generation workloads. CoreWeave has been at the center of that buildout, leveraging both debt and equity markets alongside customer commitments to fund its expansion. Given the ongoing spending in AI now and for the foreseeable future, CoreWeave's stock could have a lot of upside given the important role it plays in the industry. There is admittedly some risk with the company as it isn't profitable and has a high debt load. But for investors who want to profit from the growth in AI spend, CoreWeave may be the AI stock with the most long-term upside. If you can stomach the risk and volatility that comes with it, it may be worth hanging on to for the long haul. Intel\u2019s execution missteps have led to substantial market share losses in data center CPUs. CoreWeave is the leading cloud platform for artificial intelligence (AI) workloads. 10 stocks we like better than Intel \u203a Nvidia (NASDAQ: NVDA) sits at the center of the artificial intelligence (AI) boom by supplying customers with critical data center hardware and software. Given its deep insight into the artificial intelligence economy, keeping tabs on where Nvidia invests money can be a good way to find emerging winners As of December, Nvidia had 74% of its portfolio in two AI infrastructure stocks: 61% in Intel (NASDAQ: INTC) and 13% in CoreWeave (NASDAQ: CRWV) . But most Wall Street analysts think only one of those stocks is worth buying at current prices. Will AI create the world's first trillionaire? Our team just released a report on the one little-known company, called an \"Indispensable Monopoly\" providing the critical technology Nvidia and Intel both need. Continue \u00bb Among 50 analysts, Intel has an average target price of $54.50 per share. That implies 13% downside from its current share price of $63. Among 35 analysts, CoreWeave has an average target price of $125 per share. That implies 13% upside from its current share price of $110. Of course, investors should never assume Wall Street's short-term forecasts are accurate. It's more important to understand a company's long-term growth prospects before buying or selling shares.     "content": "CoreWeave: $21 Billion Expanded Agreement With Meta To Scale AI Infrastructure Through 2032 VC PE M&A Asset Management Law Firms Profiles Contact \u00d7 Search Pulse 2.0 CoreWeave: $21 Billion Expanded Agreement With Meta To Scale AI Infrastructure Through 2032 By Amit Chowdhry \u2022 Apr 12, 2026 CoreWeave: $21 Billion Expanded Agreement With Meta To Scale AI Infrastructure Through 2032 CoreWeave announced a long-term expansion of its partnership with Meta Platforms, securing an agreement valued at approximately $21 billion to provide AI cloud capacity through December 2032. The deal builds on the companies\u2019 existing relationship and reflects accelerating demand for high-performance infrastructure to support large-scale artificial intelligence workloads. Under the agreement, Meta will leverage CoreWeave\u2019s AI cloud platform to scale inference workloads as it continues to expand its AI development and deployment capabilities. The dedicated infrastructure will be deployed across multiple locations, incorporating early implementations of the NVIDIA Vera Rubin platform. This distributed architecture is designed to enhance performance, resilience, and scalability for Meta\u2019s AI operations. The agreement underscores a broader industry trend, as enterprises and technology leaders increasingly invest in specialized AI infrastructure to support more complex models and compute-intensive applications. CoreWeave, which went public on Nasdaq in March 2025, positions itself as a purpose-built cloud platform for AI, providing infrastructure, tools, and technical expertise to support organizations ranging from startups to global enterprises.     "title": "CoreWeave expands AI infrastructure agreements with Anthropic and Meta - EdTech Innovation Hub",
    "link": "https://news.google.com/rss/articles/CBMie0FVX3lxTE12ZXNTLU0yNV9vOGtuamF5Z0NLbkR3Tlo3NE5jYVdXQWRHeXFCd1l2N2d3VEhOdXVMYmJiQ0p3NjJrbjBfejVmQUxrQ3VOdXJvZ0Zwbm5ramNYNFNpbHRpWVRWeTNGNXBnTUFRQWFiNWw2bVIzWFBnU0pHaw?oc=5",
    "published": "2026-04-14T00:00:01Z",
    "content": "CoreWeave expands AI infrastructure deals with Anthropic and Meta | ETIH EdTech News \u2014 EdTech Innovation Hub Skip to Content Open Menu Close Menu Download the ETIH Magazine ETIH Innovation Awards 2026 News Comment Interviews Contact Us About Us Our sister title RTIH Newsletter Sign Up Advertise With ETIH Terms and Conditions 0 0 Download the ETIH Magazine ETIH Innovation Awards 2026 News Comment Interviews Contact Us About Us Our sister title RTIH Newsletter Sign Up Advertise With ETIH Terms and Conditions 0 0 Open Menu Close Menu Download the ETIH Magazine ETIH Innovation Awards 2026 News Comment Interviews Contact Us About Us Our sister title RTIH Newsletter Sign Up Advertise With ETIH Terms and Conditions CoreWeave expands AI infrastructure agreements with Anthropic and Meta AI Cloud solutions 14 Apr Written By Emma Thompson New multi-year deals highlight growing demand for AI cloud capacity as model deployment and inference workloads scale. CoreWeave scales AI cloud infrastructure through new agreements with Anthropic and Meta CoreWeave confirms a multi-year agreement with Anthropic to support the development and deployment of its Claude family of AI models. The rollout will begin later this year, with compute capacity brought online in phases. Anthropic will use CoreWeave\u2019s cloud platform to run production-scale workloads. The company joins a growing group of AI model providers using CoreWeave infrastructure, with nine of the ten largest providers now on the platform. Michael Intrator, Co-founder, CEO, and Chairman at CoreWeave, says: \u201cAI is no longer just about infrastructure, it\u2019s about the platforms that turn models into real-world impact. We\u2019re excited to work with Anthropic at the center of where models are put to work and performance in production shows up. It\u2019s exactly the kind of real-world deployment of AI that CoreWeave was built for.\u201d The agreement will initially focus on a phased deployment, with potential to expand over time as demand increases. Expanded Meta partnership CoreWeave has also expanded its existing partnership with Meta through a long-term agreement valued at approximately $21 billion. The deal runs through December 2032 and will support Meta\u2019s AI development and inference workloads. The infrastructure will be deployed across multiple locations, including early use of NVIDIA\u2019s Vera Rubin platform. The distributed setup is designed to support performance, resilience, and scalability as workloads grow. Intrator adds: \u201cThis is another example that leading companies are choosing CoreWeave\u2019s AI cloud to run their most demanding workloads.\u201d Rising demand for AI infrastructure The announcements reflect continued growth in demand for high-performance AI infrastructure, particularly as organizations move from experimentation to production deployment. CoreWeave positions its platform around performance and efficiency for modern AI workloads, including benchmark performance in MLPerf and recognition in independent evaluations of AI cloud systems. The scale of the Meta agreement, alongside new partnerships such as Anthropic, points to increasing pressure on infrastructure providers to deliver compute capacity that can support large-scale AI models across developers, startups, and enterprise environments. Both Meta and Anthropic signed their deals with CoreWeave last week, with the hope of securing access to AI cloud capability. CoreWeave is a US-based company that specialises in providing cloud-based GPU infrastructure to AI developers and enterprises. Its technology stack is designed especially for modern AI workloads. The agreements reflect the trajectory of the industry, where high-performance infrastructure is increasingly needed to support large-scale I workloads. Meta coughs up US$21bn For approximately US$21bn, CoreWeave will provide AI cloud capacity through December 2032 for Meta. The dedicated capacity will be deployed across multiple locations and will include some of the initial deployments of the NVIDIA Vera Rubin platform . The leader builders of AI are choosing us and doubling down.\u201d Anthropic locks in CoreWeave will also support the development and deployment of Anthropic\u2019s Claude family of AI models, including Project Glasswing , an ambitious and urgent effort to secure critical software. \u201cAI is no longer just about infrastructure, it\u2019s about the platforms that turn models into real-world impact,\u201d Mike says. \u201cWe\u2019re excited to work with Anthropic at the centre of where models are put to work and performance in production shows up. It\u2019s exactly the kind of real-world deployment of AI that CoreWeave was built for.\u201d The multi-year agreement will bring compute online starting later this year. It will begin with a phased infrastructure roll-out with the potential to expand over time. CoreWeave will provide the cloud infrastructure for Anthropic's Claude apps.     "content": "IREN stock analysis amid CoreWeave, Nebius market share gains \u2014 TradingView News Search Products Community Markets Brokers More EN Get started News / Invezz / IREN stock analysis amid CoreWeave, Nebius market share gains IREN stock analysis amid CoreWeave, Nebius market share gains 2 min read IREN IREN stock price has rebounded in the past few days, moving from a low of $30 on March 30 to the current $40. It remains down by 50% from its highest point in 2025. So, will the stock continue rising as top companies like CoreWeave and Nebius extend their market share? CoreWeave and Nebius are gaining their market share IREN stock price has remained under pressure in the past few weeks and is trailing other popular companies like CoreWeave and Nebius, which are slowly becoming the two biggest players in the industry. For example, CoreWeave has continued to attract customers, including the recent expansion of its deal with Meta Platforms. The company ended the last quarter with a revenue backlog of over $60 billion. Nebius, another NVIDIA-backed company, has continued gaining momentum in the past few months. For example, it recently inked a $27 billion deal with Meta Platforms, one of the biggest deals in the industry. IREN, on the other hand, has not received any major deal with a hyperscaler company this year. To date, its biggest deal was the $9.7 billion partnership with Microsoft, which was announced last year. The stock has also lagged behind its top rivals because of its growing debt load. Its total debt has jumped to over $3.6 billion from $963 million in June last year. The company has continued to boost its debt this year. For example, it raised $2.3 billion in December and has brought in over $9.3 billion in the past eight months through debt, customer prepayments, GPU leasing, and convertible notes. IREN has also announced a $6 billion At-the-Market (ATM) equity offering program to fund the purchase of 150k GPUs. This means that the number of outstanding shares will continue rising, diluting its investors. The other challenge is that other companies continue to enter the AI data center industry. Some of these companies are companies like Riot Platforms, Mara Holdings, and Cipher Mining. These concerns explain why investors have continued to short the company, with the short interest rising to 16%. On the positive side, the company is in a growing industry, with the prices of GPU rentals rising by 40% in the last 60 days. As such, even without large hyperscalers, the company\u2019s growth is expected to keep growing. Analysts expect the upcoming report to show that its revenue rose by 48% in the last quarter to $219 million. For the year, the revenue is expected to jump to $1.01 billion and $2.09 billion this year and the next one. These numbers will represent a 97% YoY and 193% YoY growth. Trading volume on Tuesday more than doubled the daily average, and bullish sentiment is evident in the options market, where the put-call ratio fell to 0.55. More than 70% of covering analysts maintain a bullish stance, with a consensus price target of $80\u2014implying upside potential of about 130% from current levels. As of April 13, eleven analysts have issued buy ratings. While Cantor Fitzgerald trimmed its target from $82 to $61 on April 9, its message stayed constructive, calling AI infrastructure \"an attractive investment area.\" The pullback to the March low of $30 seems to have been digested for now. The company\u2019s strategic pivot from a pure-play Bitcoin miner to a provider of AI cloud services is central to this bullish thesis. A landmark $9.7 billion agreement with Microsoft to supply computing capacity through the end of 2026 underpins this shift. To meet demand, IREN is aggressively scaling its capabilities, having recently finalized the purchase of over 50,000 Nvidia B300 graphics processing units. Its long-term ambition is to grow this fleet to 150,000 units. Should investors sell immediately? Or is it worth buying IREN? Nebius Group ( NBIS ) and Iren Limited ( IREN ) are chasing the same tailwind: surging demand for GPU compute. The question is which one has stronger analyst conviction. Today, Nebius operates a full-stack AI cloud \u2014 think GPU clusters, managed storage, and developer tools \u2014 targeting AI start-ups and enterprises that need compute but can\u2019t afford to build their own. At a Morgan Stanley conference in March 2026, Volozh described the ambition plainly: the company is targeting nearly one gigawatt of active power this year and has a pipeline reserved for up to three gigawatts. \u201cDemand far exceeds our supply,\u201d Nebius Chief Revenue Officer Marc Boroditsky told the audience, according to the conference transcript. Iren is an Australian-founded company that spent seven years as a Bitcoin miner before pivoting hard into AI cloud hosting. It now operates 810 megawatts of existing data centers \u2014 predominantly air-cooled, which is increasingly in demand \u2014 and has secured a $9.7 billion AI contract with Microsoft. historically bullish signal Emma Duncan Managing Editor Apr 14, 2026 at 1:48 PM IREN Data center stock IREN has short squeeze potential and technical support in place Data center stock IREN Ltd (NASDAQ:IREN) is up 9.1% to trade at $46.97 today, headed for an eighth-straight win as part of the broader tech sector rally. IREN is up 715% year over year, but has taken a 38% haircut off its Nov. 5 all-time high of $76.87. This recent rally could have staying power, with IREN clearing a historically bullish trendline. The trendline in question is the stock's 50-day moving average. Per Schaeffer's Senior Quantitative Analyst Rocky White, this \u201ccrossover\u201d event happened six other times over the last 10 years, after which IREN was higher one month later at least half of the time, averaging a whopping 33.7% gain. From its current perch, a move higher of this magnitude would put the shares at their highest level of 2026. Although calls outnumber puts on an absolute basis, bears have been circling the former blockchain maven. IREN sports a 50-day put/call volume ratio at the International Securities Exchange (ISE), Chicago Board Options Exchange (CBOE), and NASDAQ OMX PHLX (PHLX) that sits higher than 99% of annual readings. Should this pessimism begin to unwind, it could trigger more tailwinds."

Amazon recently said it would invest $25 billion into building data centers in Mississippi. Alphabet announced a $40 billion investment in Texas that will go toward building additional AI data centers. So money is flowing into the industry, but there is one giant problem: It's easy to start building an AI data center, but it's a lot harder to finish the job. Bloomberg reported that half of U.S. data center projects have been delayed or canceled due to supply shortages and reliance on Chinese imports for key components. Will AI create the world's first trillionaire? Our team just released a report on the one little-known company, called an \"Indispensable Monopoly\" providing the critical technology Nvidia and Intel both need. IREN The more gigawatts of power a data center company can secure for its servers, the more valuable it becomes in the AI buildout phase. IREN (NASDAQ: IREN) has secured more than 4.5 gigawatts of renewable energy for its AI data centers after acquiring a 1.6-gigawatt site in Oklahoma. The firm now holds 527,686 shares, representing roughly 0.19% of the company, valued at approximately $19.93 million. Not all major investors moved in the same direction. Invesco Ltd. grew its IREN holdings by 45.9% during the second quarter, reaching 2,974,674 shares valued at $43.34 million, while NewEdge Advisors expanded its position by 245.0% over the same period. In the fourth quarter, Wells Fargo and Jane Street Group significantly reduced their holdings, while Marex Group and BNP Paribas moved in the opposite direction. Despite the miss, Wall Street analysts hold a Moderate Buy consensus on the stock with an average price target of $70.08. IREN shares opened at $43.07 today, well below the 52-week high of $76.87 but substantially above the 52-week low of $5.24. Fierce competition and a growing debt load add pressure IREN operates in a rapidly crowding field. Rivals CoreWeave and Nebius , both backed by NVIDIA , have accelerated their market positions. Nebius recently secured a $27 billion deal with Meta Platforms, while CoreWeave ended its latest quarter with a revenue backlog exceeding $60 billion. IREN\u2019s largest active partnership remains a $9.7 billion deal with Microsoft announced last year, and the company has yet to land a comparable hyperscaler contract in 2026. Debt has climbed steeply, rising to $3.6 billion from $963 million in June 2025, driven by capital expenditure on hardware. See our latest analysis for IREN. After concerns around the US$6b at-the-market equity program and execution risks in its AI pivot led to a roughly 20% slide over a recent five-session stretch, the latest multi billion dollar AI cloud contracts and GPU purchase updates have coincided with a 3.05% 1 day and 5.92% 7 day share price return. The CEO said that even with chip supply and energy in place, bottlenecks remain due to the physical constraints of deploying AI data centers. The comments highlight that the race to expand AI capacity is constrained by real world buildout limits, not just technology or software. For you as an investor, the message is that AI growth is not only a story about GPUs and power contracts. IREN, which sits in the AI and data center ecosystem, is pointing to limits tied to construction timelines, permitting, and on the ground deployment. That context helps frame why some AI infrastructure projects may take longer or carry more execution risk than headlines about chip orders suggest. These remarks may also influence how you think about IREN's long term role and risk profile relative to other AI exposed names. If physical buildout constraints persist, differences in site selection, project management, and capital allocation could matter more for companies like IREN over time than simple capacity targets or theoretical demand projections. Follow 24/7 Wall St. on Google This post may contain links from our sponsors and affiliates, and Flywheel Publishing may receive compensation for actions taken through them. \u200b How to Add Us to Google News Sending You to Google News in 3 \u00a9 Gorodenkoff / Shutterstock.com Nebius Group ( NASDAQ:NBIS ) has delivered 681% gains over the past year, and the neocloud buildout continues to attract attention. The buildout is real, execution is real, and the opportunity appears to dwarf what the market has priced in. Reason 1: The Revenue Trajectory Is Unlike Anything Seen in the Sector When a company posts $227.7 million in Q4 2025 revenue, up 503.6% year-over-year, analysts pay attention. When it exits the year with an annualized run-rate of $1.25 billion and guides for $7 billion to $9 billion ARR by end of 2026, it warrants a closer look. Full-year 2025 revenue came in at $529.8 million, up 350.89% year-over-year. The AI cloud segment alone generated $214.2 million in Q4, representing 94% of total group revenue. CEO Arkady Volozh said it plainly on the Q4 earnings call: \u201cEverything we build, we sell.\u201d Reason 2: The EBITDA Inflection Tells Us the Business Model Works Adjusted EBITDA strips out aggressive infrastructure depreciation and shows whether the core business is economically viable, a more relevant lens given Nebius\u2019s heavy investment phase. In Q4 2025, group adjusted EBITDA turned positive for the first time at $15 million, compared to a loss of $63.9 million a year earlier. The AI cloud segment reached a 24% adjusted EBITDA margin in Q4, up from 19% in Q3. Operating leverage is visible: cost of revenue declined from 60% to 30% of revenue year-over-year in Q4 2025. Management guides for a group adjusted EBITDA margin of approximately 40% for full-year 2026. That is a company scaling toward profitability at an accelerating pace. Reason 3: The Moat Is Contractual, Not Just Narrative Nebius has locked in demand before"

Advertisement Approach 1: Nebius Group Discounted Cash Flow (DCF) Analysis A Discounted Cash Flow, or DCF, model takes projected future cash flows and discounts them back to what they could be worth today using a required rate of return. It is a way of translating long term cash flow expectations into a single present value per share. For Nebius Group, the model uses a 2 Stage Free Cash Flow to Equity approach based on cash flow projections in US$. The latest twelve month free cash flow is a loss of $3,545.57 million. Analyst estimates and extrapolations then feed into ten year projections that move from further free cash flow losses in the near term to a projected free cash flow of $4,780 million in 2030, with additional extrapolated figures through 2035. Bringing all of those projected cash flows back to today gives an estimated intrinsic value of US$627.77 per share. Compared to the current share price of US$154.56, the DCF output suggests that Nebius Group is about 75.4% undervalued on this model. Result: UNDERVALUED Our Discounted Cash Flow (DCF) analysis suggests Nebius Group is undervalued by 75.4%.     "content": "Nebius Pipeline Growth Supports Strong 2026 Revenue Outlook Search Newsletters Editions Home Latest Business News Money & Markets Tech & Innovation A.I. Lifestyle Leadership \u2709\ufe0f Emails \ud83c\udfa7 Podcasts Untitled Nebius Pipeline Growth Supports Strong 2026 Revenue Outlook NBIS' AI cloud boom pushes pipeline toward $4B, with longer contracts, tight capacity and strong demand shaping its 2026 outlook. Originally published on Zacks Investment Research Share to X Share to Facebook Share to Reddit Share to Email Share to Link Share to X Share to Facebook Share to Reddit Share to Email Share to Link Add Quartz on Google Nebius Group N.V. NBIS is witnessing strong momentum in its business pipeline, reinforcing confidence in its growth outlook for 2026 as demand for AI cloud services continues to accelerate. On the last earnings call, the company reported that its pipeline is expanding significantly, supported by sustained customer interest and increasing adoption across both AI-native companies and enterprise clients. This robust demand environment has led to consistent capacity sellouts, including the latter half of 2025 and into early 2026, highlighting a continued imbalance between supply and demand. Pipeline expansion is being supported by stronger customer commitments and sustained growth across both new and existing accounts. The company noted a significant increase in contract durations, with new agreements extending by nearly 50% in the fourth quarter of 2025, reflecting a shift toward longer-term engagements. Pricing trends have remained stable across GPU offerings, including older-generation units, supported by consistently high utilization. At the same time, AI-native firms are rapidly scaling their operations, evolving into enterprise-level customers with increasing compute needs, while established enterprises continue to embed AI deeper into their core business processes. Related Content Volkswagen is launching a revamped ID.3 Neo electric hatchback in Europe Microsoft is taking over OpenAI's planned Norway Stargate data center Improving deal dynamics are contributing to the strength of the pipeline. There is also a growing trend of customers securing capacity in advance, including through prepayments, to ensure access amid tight supply conditions. Reflecting this continued momentum, the company indicated that its pipeline creation in early 2026 is on track to surpass $4 billion, supported by steady demand and ongoing sales execution. Backed by these trends, Nebius reiterated its confidence in its 2026 targets. The company maintained its annualized run-rate revenue (ARR) guidance of $7 billion to $9 billion for the year. The company accelerated its capacity plans, announcing nine new data centers and securing more than 2 gigawatts of contracted power, with expectations to exceed 3 gigawatts. Nebius remains on track to deliver 800 megawatts to 1 gigawatt of available data center capacity by year-end 2026. With a strong pipeline, expanding capacity and continued demand, Nebius remains well-positioned to deliver on its growth plans. For 2026, the company expects revenue to be between $3 billion and $3.4 billion. The company anticipates the group adjusted EBITDA margin to be approximately 40%.     "content": "Why Nebius Group (NBIS) Is Up 25.3% After Announcing a New Finnish AI Factory Build - Simply Wall St News Home Portfolios Watchlist Community Discover Screener Sign Up or Login Home Portfolios Watchlist Community Discover Screener Advertisement United States / Software / NasdaqGS:NBIS Why Nebius Group (NBIS) Is Up 25.3% After Announcing a New Finnish AI Factory Build April 10, 2026 Simply Wall St Reviewed by Sasha Jovanovic Nebius Group recently announced it is building a new AI factory in Lappeenranta, Finland, with up to 310 MW of capacity, following the expansion of its M\u00e4nts\u00e4l\u00e4 site to 75 MW and reinforcing its goal of more than 3 GW of contracted power by the end of 2026. The Lappeenranta campus is designed around energy-efficient liquid cooling, heat recovery for the local district heating network, and low\u2011carbon power, highlighting how Nebius is tying large\u2011scale AI infrastructure growth to regional economic benefits and sustainability. We\u2019ll now examine how this major Finnish AI factory build, alongside Nebius\u2019s broader capacity ambitions, reshapes the company\u2019s investment narrative. Rare earth metals are the new gold rush. Find out which 27 stocks are leading the charge . Advertisement Nebius Group Investment Narrative Recap To own Nebius, you need to believe that large, long-term AI infrastructure contracts can justify heavy build outs and volatile earnings. The Lappeenranta AI factory reinforces Nebius\u2019s \u201cscale first\u201d thesis and supports its contracted power target, but it also sharpens the key short term tension between capital intensity and the market\u2019s patience with losses and dilution. For now, this news mainly reinforces, rather than changes, that central risk reward trade off. The Lappeenranta plan sits alongside Nebius\u2019s US$27 billion, five year AI infrastructure agreement with Meta, which is currently the most important commercial proof point behind its capacity build. While the Meta deal anchors demand for future sites, including Finland, it also raises concentration risk and heightens execution pressure: Nebius has committed to deliver substantial capacity from 2027, and delays or cost overruns at new campuses could directly affect that flagship contract. Yet beneath the growth story, investors should still pay attention to the risk that heavy spending and insider selling could... Read the full narrative on Nebius Group (it's free!) Nebius Group's narrative projects $15.2 billion revenue and $1.7 billion earnings by 2029. This requires 206.4% yearly revenue growth and about a $1.7 billion earnings increase from $29.0 million today. Uncover how Nebius Group's forecasts yield a $165.85 fair value , a 22% upside to its current price. Exploring Other Perspectives NBIS 1-Year Stock Price Chart The most bearish analysts were already assuming Nebius might not be profitable for at least three years even with revenue reaching about US$2.8 billion by 2028, so against that backdrop this Finland build out could either ease worries about long term demand or deepen concerns about rising capex and contract concentration depending on how you weigh those risks. CTech 09:26, 09.04.26 AI21 Labs is in acquisition talks once again, this time with AI infrastructure company Nebius, according to a report by The Information, months after earlier negotiations with Nvidia failed to result in a deal. Calcalist previously reported that the Israeli startup had held advanced discussions with Nvidia at a reported valuation of $2 billion to $3 billion, above its last known valuation of $1.4 billion from 2023. Those talks reached senior levels but ultimately did not materialize into a transaction. 1 View gallery AI21 Labs founders. ( Photo: Orel Cohen ) In January, co-founder and chairman Amnon Shashua confirmed that AI21 was engaged in discussions with Nvidia as well as other potential buyers, indicating that the company was formally exploring strategic options. Founded in 2017 by Shashua, Professor Yoav Shoham and Ori Goshen, AI21 was among the early companies developing large-scale language models. However, it has struggled in recent years to keep pace with leading AI developers such as OpenAI and Anthropic. The company has since narrowed its focus to enterprise applications, emphasizing accuracy and reliability. It discontinued development of its consumer product, Wordtune, and now centers on offerings such as its Maestro platform and a recently introduced reasoning model. Annual revenue is estimated at around $50 million. Related articles: \u201cThere are talks with Nvidia and others\u201d: Shashua confirms AI21 acquisition discussions AI21 never closed its reported $300 million round as Nvidia weighs an acquisition Nvidia in advanced talks to acquire AI21 in $2-3 billion deal focused on talent AI21 employs roughly 200 people, down from about 260 in 2023, after scaling back expansion plans. Much of the interest in the company has been attributed to its workforce, which includes highly trained AI researchers. Acquisition Rumor Sparks New Stock Surge By Rich Duprey Published Apr 10, 12:10PM EDT Quick Read Nebius Group ( NBIS ) is in talks to acquire AI21 Labs, a $1.4 billion-valued Israeli AI startup with $50M annual revenue and a 200-person research team, to expand beyond GPU rental into higher-margin software services. Nebius posted 500% YoY quarterly growth with $529.8M trailing 12-month revenue and $3.68B cash on hand, positioning it to fund both the AI21 acquisition and its $16-20B data center capex plan. Model-development costs climbing into the billions are driving consolidation among AI infrastructure players, with Nebius seeking to build a full-stack platform after Nvidia walked away from AI21. The analyst who called NVIDIA in 2010 just named his top 10 AI stocks. Get them here FREE . Last year Nvidia ( NASDAQ:NVDA | NVDA Price Prediction ) walked away from advanced talks to buy Israeli startup AI21 Labs at a $2 billion to $3 billion valuation. Now another buyer has stepped in. Reports say Nebius Group ( NASDAQ:NBIS ) is in discussions to acquire the company, and the news sent its stock soaring. Investors who own the stock or watch the AI-cloud space want one clear answer: does this move turn Nebius into a full-stack contender, or is it just another high-priced gamble in a sector already priced for perfection? The Rumored Deal\u2019s Details The Information reported Wednesday that Nebius entered talks to buy AI21 Labs, months after Nvidia\u2019s negotiations collapsed. Founded in 2017, AI21 once ranked among the early leaders in large language models. It has since pivoted to enterprise tools such as the Maestro platform and a new reasoning model after discontinuing its consumer product Wordtune. Annual revenue sits at roughly $50 million. Its last disclosed valuation was $1.4 billion in 2023; Nvidia\u2019s earlier interest reportedly reached $2 billion to $3 billion. Sources told The Information"

Search Blog Markets Insights Apr 13, 2026 Nebius Group: AI Infrastructure Powerhouse Sees Stock Surge and Major Contracts Nebius Group , trading on the NASDAQ, has emerged as a neocloud company specializing in constructing data centers designed for artificial intelligence workloads. According to a report from Yahoo Finance , the company was formed after the former Yandex N.V. divested its Russian business assets to concentrate on AI infrastructure. The company's stock has seen a significant increase in value since the start of the prior year. This surge in investor interest followed financial backing from Nvidia and the securing of substantial contracts. Analysts have shown optimism, with a median price target indicating potential for further growth from its current trading level. Role in AI Infrastructure Nebius builds specialized, GPU-powered data centers, sometimes termed AI factories, to address computing resource constraints faced by AI developers and hyperscalers. This model allows client companies to expand their capacity more rapidly than by constructing their own facilities. The company has secured multiyear agreements with Microsoft and Meta Platforms . The total value of these contracts is reported to be substantial, demonstrating significant demand from major technology firms. Financial Prospects and Strategic Moves These major contracts are expected to drive considerable revenue growth for Nebius. The company concluded the previous year with reported total revenue in the hundreds of millions. Analyst projections indicate a sharp increase for the current year and an even larger figure for the following year. Nvidia's multi-billion dollar investment in the company is viewed as a strategic advantage, potentially granting Nebius earlier access to future hardware platforms. Furthermore, Nebius recently moved to acquire an agentic AI search company. This acquisition, valued in the hundreds of millions, is seen as an effort to strengthen its position within a specific and promising segment of the AI market, which research firms estimate could grow significantly over the next ten years. 1. In this round, Nebius continued to demonstrate strong results for AI inference workloads running on the latest NVIDIA platforms. Our submission includes systems built on NVIDIA Blackwell and Blackwell Ultra GPUs \u2014 NVIDIA HGX B200, NVIDIA HGX B300 and the rack-scale NVIDIA GB300 NVL72 system. The potential deal would mark a move beyond Nebius\u2019s current focus on AI infrastructure into proprietary AI software and platform offerings. This M&A development has not featured in recent coverage that has centered on Nebius\u2019s data center plans, analyst attention, and valuation scenarios. Nebius Group enters these reported talks with AI21 Labs after a period of very strong share price performance, with NasdaqGS:NBIS up 33.2% over the past week and 29.4% over the past month. The stock is also up 61.2% year to date and has delivered a very large return over the past year from its current share price of $144.97. That backdrop means any confirmed move into AI applications is likely to be closely watched by investors already focused on Nebius\u2019s growth story. If Nebius proceeds with acquiring AI21 Labs, it would extend the business from infrastructure into higher margin AI software and platforms, broadening its addressable market. For you as an investor, key questions include how Nebius integrates AI21\u2019s technology, what this means for future spending, and whether a fuller AI stack changes how the market values NasdaqGS:NBIS over time. AI21 Labs brings large language models and its Maestro agent management product, which could sit on top of Nebius data centers and help convert contracted GPU capacity into higher-margin software and platform revenue. At the same time, the rumored purchase price of several billion dollars would add to an already heavy capital program linked to Meta and Microsoft contracts, so investors need to weigh the extra software upside against balance sheet and integration demands. AI21\u2019s earlier struggle to gain broad traction with its models also raises a question about commercial execution and how Nebius might position these products against better-known rivals. In this article NBIS Follow your favorite stocks CREATE FREE ACCOUNT Nebius unveiled plans on Tuesday to build a new AI data center in Finland, which it says will be one of Europe's largest when up-and-running, as the region races to develop the compute infrastructure needed to power the AI boom. The new facility will be based in the Finnish city of Lappeenranta with a capacity of up to 310 MW. Nebius said it expects the data center to begin initially supplying customers by 2027. \"We have been building in Finland for many years and are pleased to be expanding our presence here,\" said Nebius CEO Arkady Volozh in a statement. \"Lappeenranta represents a significant addition to our global AI infrastructure build-out, and will make a significant contribution to achieving our capacity goals.\" Europe's AI buildout It comes amid a slew of AI infrastructure announcements across Europe. French AI startup Mistral said Monday it had secured $830 million in debt financing to operate a data center near Paris. That came after a February announcement of a 1.2-billion-euro ($1.38 billion) plan to build data centers and compute capacity in Sweden. U.K. startup Nscale announced it had raised $2 billion at a $14.6 billion valuation earlier this month, with plans to develop AI data centers in its home market alongside Europe and the U.S. Meanwhile, 2025 saw MGX, Bpifrance, Mistral and Nvidia unveil plans for a 1.4 GW AI campus in France and Brookfield said it"

Three stocks are noted for their possible strong returns, though success is not assured. IonQ Quantum computing is developing as a field, with IonQ positioned as a leader due to its current status in accuracy. The company reports substantial demand for its early-stage products and has formed multiple client research partnerships. Its revenue reportedly increased significantly in the final quarter of 2025 compared to the prior year, with expectations for it to double in the following year. The quantum computing sector remains highly competitive. SoundHound AI SoundHound AI develops software that integrates generative artificial intelligence with audio recognition. Its current primary application is in restaurant drive-thru automation and in-vehicle digital assistants. The potential market for this technology is viewed as extensive, with possible future applications in any phone-based human interaction, including customer service areas in healthcare, insurance, and finance. Nebius is one of the more unusual players in the AI boom: a \"startup\" that didn't exactly start from scratch. Loading audio narration... The company emerged in 2024 from the breakup of Russian search giant Yandex . A complex transaction left the new Nebius with hundreds of employees and roughly $2.5 billion in capital, giving it a scale and war chest most new entrants can only dream of. With decades of experience building massive data centers for a huge online search business, the Nebius team brought deep know-how to the AI infrastructure race. The company builds AI data centers from the ground up. I met Nebius CEO Arkady Volozh when he was in Silicon Valley for Nvidia's GTC conference earlier this month.
